# Skills · C · part 228

852 entries, concise-answers to condense. [Index](README.md) · [Everything](../README.md)

- **concise-answers**: Instructs agents to answer questions, explain code, and provide information with extreme brevity while preserving correctness. Use when the goal is to inform rather than modify — "how does X work", "why is this failing", "where is Y defined", "what's the best way to do Z", "explain this function". Trigger for any question-answering, code explanation, debugging guidance, architecture questions, or API/library usage. Enforces short, direct, read-only responses. ([baxy5/R2](https://github.com/baxy5/R2/tree/HEAD/skills/concise-answers))
- **concise-ask-mode**: Shapes answers to questions about code, errors, architecture, or tools. Use when answering in ask mode (read-only Q&A, no edits): explain, "why", "how does", "what is", "which is better", or review questions. Gives the answer first, in short, simple technical English. ([ahautelman/autospec-faultline](https://github.com/ahautelman/autospec-faultline/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/concise-ask-mode))
- **concise-changelog-entries**: Keep changelog entries (`.changelog/unreleased/`) to a single concise sentence. Use when adding or editing changelog entries. ([0xMiden/protocol](https://github.com/0xMiden/protocol/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/concise-changelog-entries) · ★135)

- **concise-code**: Keep a change small and in the existing idiom - size the plan and weigh smaller alternatives before writing it, reuse the mechanism that already owns the behavior instead of adding a parallel one, and simplify the diff before handoff. - **condensation-check**: Works out where water condenses inside a wall, roof, or floor assembly: the temperature at every layer boundary from the layer R-values and the design conditions, the dew point of the indoor air from its temperature and relative humidity, and which boundary is the first to fall below it. Then walks the layers in order of vapor permeance to flag a vapor-tight layer sitting on the cold side of the insulation, or two of them sealing the assembly from both directions. - **condensation-film**: Film condensation — Nusselt film theory, Chato correlation, condensate flooding, effect of vapor velocity, non-condensable gases, enhancement methods, tube vs. flat plate, TEMA condenser. ([MP-AI-20/mechanical-engineering-skills](https://github.com/MP-AI-20/mechanical-engineering-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/condensation-film) · ★13)
- **condensation-sequence-simulation**: Use when you need equilibrium condensation sequences or 50% condensation temperatures (Tc) of rock-forming elements (Al, Ti, Ca, Mg, Fe, Si) for a given stellar abundance pattern and disk pressure, e.g. reproducing Table 2 benchmarks at p = 1e-4 bar. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-astronomy](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-astronomy/tree/HEAD/collections/astronomy/v1/leaves/condensation-sequence-simulation))
- **condensation-temperature-derivation**: Use when a T-p-dependent condensation simulation (Gibbs minimisation over gas + pure-condensate species) has been run for a stellar abundance pattern at a fixed pressure (e.g. 1e-4 bar) and you need to extract and validate per-element condensation temperatures against literature benchmarks. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-astronomy](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-astronomy/tree/HEAD/collections/astronomy/v1/leaves/condensation-temperature-derivation))
- **condensation-temperature-extraction**: Use when you have run (or need to run) an ECCOplanets equilibrium condensation simulation and must convert element/molecule abundance-vs-temperature curves into per-element 50% condensation temperatures for validation or composition analysis. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-astronomy](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-astronomy/tree/HEAD/collections/astronomy/v1/leaves/condensation-temperature-extraction))
- **condensation-temperature-pressure-sensitivity-analysis**: Use when you need to determine how equilibrium condensation temperatures of rock-forming elements depend on disk pressure (e.g. testing whether element volatility defined at Solar System pressure applies to other disks), or to reproduce a ∆Tc vs. pressure sensitivity table. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-astronomy](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-astronomy/tree/HEAD/collections/astronomy/v1/leaves/condensation-temperature-pressure-sensitivity-analysis))
- **condensation-temperature-simulation**: Use when you need condensation temperatures (Tc) of planet-building elements for a stellar abundance pattern, or when quantifying how ∆Tc responds to varying disk pressure, metallicity, or element ratios like C/O from 0.1 to 0.7. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-astronomy](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-astronomy/tree/HEAD/collections/astronomy/v1/leaves/condensation-temperature-simulation))
